# Record 20260804-211600-f599a65

- **Record ID**: 20260804-211600-f599a65
- **Claim**: #8 (design-input claim feeding two spec lines) -- how much does `vdd_vco` move the ring VCO's frequency, statically and in a transient, and what timing error does a realistic supply disturbance therefore inject? DR-001's Consequences section names this the accepted risk of the current-starved single-ended ring and states the requirement in terms this record answers: "#8 must produce a supply-step/supply-noise jitter testbench, not just a clean-supply Kvco sweep. This is the number most likely to force a supersede." Feeds #14's supply-sensitivity budget and #13's jitter budget. Spec-line references are placeholders pending ratification (#1): `spec/pll.md#supply-sensitivity`, `spec/pll.md#period-jitter`.

- **Methodology / criteria / limitations**:
  - **Two decks, one record.** The claim is a pair: a *static* pushing deck (`tb_vco_pushing.spice`, seven VCO copies at seven supplies) and a *transient* deck (`tb_vco_supply_jitter.spice`, a quiet / stepped / rippled copy). The jitter numbers are not interpretable without the pushing numbers -- the quasi-static model that says whether they project to other ripple frequencies is written in terms of the pushing coefficient -- so they are one record, minted through the harness's `phases` key. Every PVT point of the shared grid runs both decks; each deck's log is `<phase>_<corner-id>.log`.
  - **Static pushing measurement criterion**: seven independent VCO copies, one per supply point (2.97 .. 3.63 V in 0.11 V steps), in a single transient. Each copy carries its own supply source, its own ammeter and its own band-code sources **referenced to that copy's rail** -- a shared logic-level band code would leave the band-select pass gates of the higher-rail copies partly on and corrupt the measurement. Frequency is taken at each copy's buffered `CLK` over four whole cycles after a settle window of >= 4 estimated periods. The per-corner pushing coefficient is a least-squares fit of f against vdd over those seven points, and `push_nl_pct` is the largest departure of the measured curve from its own straight line, as a percentage of the nominal-supply frequency -- so the record states how good the single-coefficient summary is rather than assuming it.
  - **Transient measurement criterion**: three VCO copies per run, identical except for their supply drive -- quiet (dc), stepped (0.1 V step, 1 ns edge, at the window midpoint) and rippled (0.05 V amplitude sine). Every reported number comes from **interpolated half-supply crossings of the buffered output**, extracted by `derive_supply.py` from the deck's own `jit.dat` waveform, not from `.measure`: jitter is a property of the period *sequence* and `.measure` reports scalars. The window always holds four whole ripple periods and at least 64 cycles, so a peak-to-peak TIE is a real peak-to-peak.
  - **The quiet copy is the noise floor, not a result.** Any period variation it shows is the transient solver's own local truncation error at these settings (`reltol = 1e-4`, max timestep 1/(200 f_osc)). The record reports the stepped and rippled numbers **against** that floor: `floor_margin_x` is the rippled TIE RMS divided by that point's own quiet TIE RMS, and it is a checked quantity, so a record in which any supply-induced number is not comfortably above the floor fails rather than quoting it.
  - **Quasi-static model, and why it is checked**: a ring VCO's supply response is flat well beyond any supply-noise frequency of interest, so f(t) = f0 + K_vdd*v_ripple(t) predicts TIE_pp = K*A/(pi*f_rip*f0) and period modulation 2*T0*K*A/f0. Both predictions are computed from the *measured transient* K_vdd and compared with the measured jitter at three ripple frequencies (f_osc/4, /16, /32). Agreement is what licenses projecting these numbers to ripple frequencies that were not simulated; disagreement means they cannot be projected, and the record says so instead.
  - **The window estimator, and why it is also the stimulus.** Both decks size their transient window in-deck from a calibrated frequency estimate (see the fragment headers). For the pushing deck that is a pure runtime knob -- an estimate wrong enough to lose a rising edge makes the `.measure` fail and the point is reported failed, never silently mis-measured. For the jitter deck it is more than that: the ripple frequency is *specified* as f_osc/N, so the estimate sets the stimulus. That is why the estimator's process term reaches the deck as the `fcal` sweep axis, whose grid blocks pair each factor with the corner bundles it calibrates: a ripple defined relative to f_osc cannot be corner-blind. `fcal` is a calibration constant of the estimator, not a model corner -- the `.lib` sections are the harness's, as always. The estimator is preserved term-for-term from the pre-migration bash runner (`testbench/common.sh :: f_est`) so that this record re-runs the *same* experiment as the record it supersedes rather than a similar one.
  - **Migration self-check.** This campaign replaces `testbench/run_supply.sh`, which minted record `20260731-184845-0a12e6c` through the `sim/lib/simenv.sh` shim. The `migration_delta` derived table joins that record's own committed CSVs and reports, per quantity, the median and worst relative deviation of the migrated numbers from it over the points the two grids share. A migration whose numbers moved is a finding, not a footnote, so it is computed into the record rather than asserted in a commit message.
  - **Limitations**:
  -   - **Open loop.** No PLL is closed around this VCO here, so nothing corrects the supply-induced frequency error. The reported TIE is therefore an upper bound for supply disturbances *inside* the (not yet designed) loop bandwidth and approximately correct for disturbances above it. The closed-loop number is #12/#13's, and it cannot be produced before #9/#10 fix the loop bandwidth.
  -   - **Deterministic disturbances only.** This record injects a step and a sinusoid. It is not a random-noise (phase-noise) simulation and reports no random jitter number; `sim/README.md`'s worked example and #13 both flag ngspice transient noise as a separate, harder question.
  -   - **Schematic level.** No supply-network parasitics: the rail is driven by an ideal source through the on-chip decap only. Real bond-wire and on-die rail impedance will make the *disturbance* larger for a given external event; this record characterizes the VCO's sensitivity to a rail disturbance, not the disturbance itself. Layout extraction (#18) will also move every absolute frequency here downward.
  -   - **One control voltage.** Both decks run at Vctrl = 1.8 V, the middle of the 0.9-2.7 V window. The Vctrl dependence of f_osc is the tuning-range record's claim, not this one's.
  -   - Statistical switches: `sw_stat_global = sw_stat_mismatch = 0` (nominal per-corner skew, no Monte Carlo mismatch).
- **Statistical convention**: N/A (corner-matrix claim, not a distribution claim)
- **Result**:

  **supply_pushing_summary** (derived) — static supply pushing, per band code (deck tb_vco_pushing.spice)

  Local slope of f_osc against vdd_vco, least-squares over the seven supply points spanning 2.97-3.63 V, at every (bundle, temperature, band) combination -- 96 curves of 7 points each.
  Worst (most supply-sensitive) point: **-54.5 %/V** (-44.91 MHz/V at 82.34 MHz) at ss/-40C, band 5, Vctrl 1.8 V.
  Best: **-24.1 %/V** at ff/-40C, band 0.
  Curvature: the largest departure of any measured f(vdd) curve from its own straight-line fit is 3.41 % of f_nom, so pushing is effectively linear across the whole +/-10 % rail and a single coefficient per corner is a fair summary.
  **Where the sensitivity comes from, and why it is not a sizing error.** A current-starved ring runs at f ~ I_stage / (N * C_stage * V_swing), and V_swing *is* the supply. Even with a perfectly supply-independent starving current, f therefore carries a -1/vdd term = **-30.3 %/V** at 3.3 V. The measured median of -40.5 %/V is 1.34x that floor, i.e. the bias generator contributes only the remainder. Reducing supply pushing materially would require a regulated VCO rail or a swing-independent cell (a differential delay cell with replica-biased load), i.e. exactly the alternatives DR-001 Decision 2 considered and rejected on power and cell-count grounds -- not a resizing of this cell.

  **supply_step_response** (derived) — response to a 0.1 V supply step with a 1 ns edge, over the 63-point mandated PVT grid (band 5, Vctrl 1.8 V ~ 100 MHz class)

  The last column is the number a loop-bandwidth budget has to beat: with the loop open, a supply step of this size walks the VCO's output edge by that much timing error for every microsecond the loop takes to correct it. It is *not* a jitter spec violation on its own -- inside the loop bandwidth the PLL cancels it -- but it is the reason #10 cannot choose an arbitrarily low loop bandwidth, and it is a direct input to #14's supply-sensitivity budget.

  **supply_ripple_jitter** (derived) — sinusoidal ripple of 0.05 V amplitude (100 mV peak-to-peak, 3.0 % of the nominal rail) at f_osc/16, over the 63-point mandated PVT grid

  The smallest margin between a rippled result and its own run's quiet reference anywhere on the grid is **135x** in TIE RMS (at sf/125C/3.30V), so every supply-induced number above is a circuit result, not solver noise.
  Worst case as a fraction of the period: **2.51 % RMS period jitter** and 7.14 % peak-to-peak, at all-slow/-40C/2.97V, f_osc = 78.52 MHz.
  The draft spec line is < 1 % RMS period jitter, so **a 100 mV peak-to-peak ripple on an unregulated VCO rail is on its own sufficient to break that line** open-loop.

  **ripple_frequency_check** (derived) — does the quasi-static model reproduce the measurement, i.e. may these numbers be projected to ripple frequencies that were not simulated?

  Each row is averaged over the 3 bundle(s) that ran at every ripple frequency (all-fast, all-slow, typical), at 27 C and the nominal supply, so the ripple frequency is the only variable between rows.
  Across all 144 rippled runs the measured/predicted ratio spans 1.22 .. 2.29 (median 1.46).
  TIE scales as 1/f_ripple as the model predicts -- the *shape* is right -- but the magnitude is consistently off by that factor, so the step-derived coefficient is **not** a safe basis for projecting ripple jitter: a ring's response to a bidirectional ripple is larger than its response to a unidirectional step of the same peak-to-peak size, because f ~ 1/vdd is convex. Ripple jitter at a ripple frequency that matters must be **measured**, not extrapolated. The measured numbers stand on their own; only the extrapolation is withheld.

  **migration_delta** (derived) — numeric comparison against superseded record 20260731-184845-0a12e6c

  Every quantity the superseded record `20260731-184845-0a12e6c` published, re-measured through `sim/harness` and compared point by point: 1872 shared measurements over 13 quantities. The deltas are relative, in percent.
  The two runs are the same experiment, not merely a similar one: the decks, the seven-supply pushing axis, the 0.1 V step, the 0.05 V ripple, the window rule and the in-deck frequency estimator that sets the ripple frequency are all preserved term for term. What changed is the runner (bash + `sim/lib/simenv.sh` -> `sim/run_corners.py`), the deck composition (a `.control` block instead of a top-level `.tran`) and the extraction's host (`jitter_extract.py` -> this module, same arithmetic). A non-zero delta is therefore a finding about one of those, and is discussed in the record's Methodology field, not absorbed.

  **supply_verdict** (derived) — what this record establishes, and what it does not

  **This does not by itself supersede DR-001 Decision 2.** The measured sensitivity is what a current-starved single-ended ring is expected to have, and DR-001 accepted it explicitly with eyes open. What the number does establish is a hard constraint for #10 and #14: the VCO rail needs either regulation or a decoupling/ripple budget, and the loop bandwidth cannot be set without knowing the supply-noise spectrum. If the system cannot deliver a quiet enough rail, *that* is the condition under which DR-001 Decision 2 should be revisited, and this record is the evidence that would drive it.

  | quantity | value | where | reading |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | static supply pushing, worst corner | -54.5 %/V | ss/-40C, band 5 | characterized; 1.80x the -1/vdd swing term, so dominated by the ring's swing-vs-supply term rather than by bias-current sensitivity |
  | pushing linearity, worst curve | 3.41 % of f_nom | all 96 curves | a single pushing coefficient per corner is a fair summary |
  | supply-ripple TIE, worst corner | 3.37 ns peak-to-peak | all-slow/-40C/2.97V | DR-001's top named risk now has a number, not a caveat |
  | supply-ripple period jitter, worst corner | 2.51 % RMS of the period | all-slow/-40C/2.97V | BREAKS the draft < 1 % line, open loop, from a 100 mV peak-to-peak rail ripple alone |
  | margin over the solver's numerical floor | 135x in TIE RMS | smallest anywhere on the grid | every supply-induced number above is a circuit result, not solver noise |
